ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e2884	Mr. Finanzminister, my question is: [[/PAKT/VHG/XXI/M/M_00030/index.shtml -- 30/M]] In the light of the international criticism of EU, OECD and IMF, what are your strategic considerations and approaches for the 2001 budget?
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e2912	Please, Minister of State.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e2927	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. Mr Trattner! At present, we can all look forward to an excellent economic situation in Austria – whether this is the labour market or the employment situation with an unemployment rate of 3.4, 3.5 percent, as low as it has been for a long time. We can be pleased with excellent values, both in terms of exports and investments in Austria. We have an economic growth which is the 5 percent nominal, and we have an inflation rate that is okay. That is to say, we have an excellent economic policy starting point in which we can continue budgetary consolidation or seriously address it after the end of 1998 and 1999. That is also the objective of this Federal Government. I believe that there is an urgent need for a fundamental change in financial policy, especially if we want to ensure the quality of life of the population, as we have today at a high level, and if we want to improve it in the future. That is, if we want sustainable growth in Austria, if we want to have an excellent situation on the labour market, we also need to bring financial policy into line with it. That was the reason why I proposed to the Federal Government to achieve a balanced budget during this legislative period, so that we can still say in one financial year during this legislative period: We are no longer making new debts, in the interests of employment, in the interests of economic growth, in the interests of children and the next generation in our country. We also need this budget consolidation so that we can become proactive on the other hand, so that we can also adopt the measures set out in the Government Agreement – whether research or development, whether they are educational institutions for youth, wage reductions or family policy initiatives. If we want to put in place these active measures, we need this enhanced consolidation, and we feel obliged to do so.

On the other hand, the IMF has expressed its criticism of the budgetary situation in Austria and has explicitly criticised the absence of further consolidation progress following the consolidation success of 1996 and 1997 in the two subsequent years 1998 and 1999. The IMF also made it quite clear that the failure of the previous Federal Government was not able to put in place appropriate structural measures to continue consolidation, which he clearly saw as responsible for the fact that Austria is unfortunately God's final light in terms of consolidation in the European context.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3024	Additional question? – Mr Fink, please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3039	Mr. Minister of Finance! With regard to the excellent economic situation and the ambitious project of pressing the budget deficit to zero until the end of this legislative period, I have the following question: Has the International Monetary Fund expressed in its final report any demands or recommendations that you would have to take into account?
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3054	Please answer me, Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3070	Ladies and gentlemen! You're right: The International Monetary Fund has raised several explicit claims. First of all, the International Monetary Fund has said that we should seek a balanced budget or a slight surplus in Austria so that, in the event of economic downturn on the other hand, we can have room for counter-controlling, proactive measures, also have room to deal with the long-term problems of ageing the population, in order to be able to put in place economically active measures such as an additional wage reduction. In view of the fact that at present every eighth tax schilling has to be used to pay interest rates, the IMF has in turn limited the scope for financial policy – the interest payments currently amount to around 100 billion schilling gross per year – insisting that the government debt ratio should be reduced rapidly. That means: Reimbursement of debt in order to reduce interest payments and facilitate the achievement of structural effects. The IMF, in full agreement with our plans, has also expressed its support for the need for expenditure-side consolidation. In other words, it is first and foremost about structural reforms, about task reforms in the public sector, in order to reduce expenditure and not have to think about new burdens on the population. The IMF has explicitly called on the Austrian Federal Government to recover the old government's failure since 1998 in this legislative period and to achieve a structurally balanced budget by 2003. – These were the important demands of the IMF.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3308	Federal Minister! My supplementary question goes in the direction that I ask you to clarify to us the measures you are discussing or having in advance something that you are telling us how you want to balance.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3323	Please, Mr. Minister.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e3338	Ladies and gentlemen! Of course, for the first time, it is a very ambitious goal to achieve a balanced budget. For the first time in more than 30 years, it would be the case that this fundamental change in financial policy in Austria is being achieved. Secondly, I can tell you: Of course, we have specific considerations which I would like to present to you in the first place. Structural reforms, for example. A debate on pension reform was held yesterday in the House. I believe that a pension reform, as it was decided yesterday, is necessary if one sees that people on the one hand are longer in training and on the other hand, people are living longer thanks to God and can therefore use the pension longer, making the contribution years in total shorter. It is therefore very clear in this demographic development that what was decided yesterday is indicative if you want to ensure pensions on the basis of the payment procedure. It is also a contribution to slowing the dynamics of federal expenditure in the order of 28 billion by 2003 to around 13 billion for 2003. That means that there are more expenditure, 13 billion schilling, but there is no stronger increase in extra expenditure. That is, the discussion is not about a real reduction, but about how much the effort at the federal level is increasing. Next point: Administrative reform. You know that we have already taken decisions in the Council of Ministers in this respect, for example, with regard to the stopover. I am absolutely aware that, from my point of view, we still have to change the administrative structure attached to the monarchy in Austria – whether it is at municipal level, at the level of the district headquarters, at the national level or at the federal level. It is necessary to try, under the impression of completely new electronic means, to build a new administrative structure, which is really in the interests of the citizen, which is closely related to the citizen – and which does not lead to absurdities, to the introduction of an e-government which is also a model in Europe, but which is also based on the intention to say: We put an electronic instrument in the hands of the citizen so that he can check electronically to which bureaucratic institution he has to go to so that he can solve his problem there. Ladies and gentlemen! There is a real need to ask which institutions serve the citizen, what is useful to the citizen, and this is to be rewritten into the future. On the other hand, such a job reform, which is linked to it, also brings a very large potential for savings for taxpayers, for the population. You know we're also working on a debt repayment program. If you want to make room for it, then it must be the common goal to reduce repayments and interest rates for past debts. This year we have about 250 billion Schilling interest and repayment payments for old debt. This, of course, limits the scope, and this also shows how high it is on the other side. The tax and tax side must be so that we can finance it. We have once converted it and considered how much more could be given to the population through tax and tax reductions, we should not have to finance this burden from the past, and we have come to the amount of 7 300 s per person in employment net in Austria per month. 7 300 S net a month could be paid out more over tax and tax reductions if one did not have these burdens from the past. That is why the direction is clear: to make a debt reduction program, which will allow you to gain room on the interest side. We discussed the financial compensatory negotiations last time. I recognize that saving is not something that concerns only the federal government, but it affects all the local authorities: it concerns the municipalities, the cities, the countries, the federal government. To this end, it is a great concern for us to come to a new Stability Pact and take all the responsibility to show the population: We all strive to make a contribution to the consolidation of the State budget by all public authorities. All these measures are going, as you see, towards expenditure reductions, structural reforms, job reforms, because we want to be a government that is reformed first on the expenditure side and only when it is necessary – we try to avoid that – thinking about new revenue.

I think that such an instrument would be important and useful because it contributes to greater security in our country, but above all it also contributes to greater legal certainty for the officials acting. However, it must be clear to everyone that this is a great intervention, an indisputable and very sensitive intervention in fundamental rights. It is also indisputable that you must have such control if you make such an intervention. The new term is shorter, thus avoiding confusion with the term "criminal organisation" defined in the StGB, and thus avoiding the question of the border between "bands" and "organised crime". In addition, we have taken into account the identity ID created in 1999 in this novel and have complied with the comments made by the Data Protection Council. We have also taken into account the wishes of the judiciary in § 54, in the field of observation, and there has been a restriction on the wishes of the judiciary. In this sense, I would like to thank all the Members of the Committee on the Interior and also those who will vote in favour of this proposal here today in the House. It will ensure that organised crime can continue to be effectively and successfully combated.

This legislative initiative is designed to complement advanced risk research with developments that cause serious public security problems, and to clarify the restriction of authorisation in criminal links. It is already intended to start a risk investigation at a time when no crimes have yet been committed, but on the basis of concrete evidence, there is a likelihood that in the foreseeable future, criminal acts could be committed which would lead to a serious risk of public security. In the case of advanced risk research, only the observation of clearly defined groups is permitted. Hidden investigations using image and sound recording devices may only be allowed to prevent criminal connections if crime is expected and always taking into account proportionality. I think that no person in this House can have anything against the fact that advanced risk research is being carried out if serious public security disadvantages are feared or if crime is expected. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] This amendment of the Security Police Act is also a long-standing adjustment to the circumstances of criminal development. The steady increase in organised crime – which has already been mentioned several times today – is a fact. We must not shut down the criminal reality in the organized area, but we must combat organised crime with appropriate search and survey measures. To date, the security authority has either been established in the legal area or investigations have been made only when the fundamental rights of citizens have already been severely affected. It is always preferable to protect the fundamental rights of citizens rather than the rights of potential perpetrators, and controls and, bearing in mind the proportionality in individual cases where it is absolutely necessary to intervene in fundamental rights of people who intend to commit a crime. It is therefore a matter of greater legal certainty. This is an important and correct step towards greater legal certainty and a meaningful addition to the existing security police law. Ladies and gentlemen! It is our task to give security authorities the legal means of effectively protecting the public from criminal attacks. The Authority will only have these possibilities if it is able to develop and prepare counter-strategies effectively in advance or during the development and preparation phase. It must be our will to ensure that our security authorities are able to act in a timely manner – if possible, preventively, without any damage to the state or to the population – and to intervene in a targeted manner. On the other hand, this government proposal for an extended investigation into the security police law also concerns legal certainty for all those persons against which action is to be taken. In addition to the existing control systems, such as the Data Protection Commission and the Independent Administrative Senate, this is also used as a legal protection officer. It seems very important to me that in a rule of law a far-reaching step in the privacy of a person can be controlled and in certain cases possible. It is interesting that the opposition seems already to know that the installation of a legal protection officer, as provided for in the Government template, does not constitute sufficient control and that compliance with fundamental rights is not ensured in the case of people against whom action is being taken. I ask everyone to be more state-bearing and less suspicious!

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e15314	Mr President. Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen. I assume that all of us have great concerns about confidence in Austrian economic policy and financial policy. If you want to have such a confidence, of course a stable and solid banking scene is inseparable. The crisis of individual banks in Austria must be ours, yes, we cannot afford, if we are concerned with economic policy, financial policy nationally and internationally. In the interests of investor protection, too, we should not allow uncertainty caused by banking crises such as they are undoubtedly in Burgenland, and we should do everything we can to ensure the stability and security of the Austrian banking landscape. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I should now like to make this case of the Bank Burgenland a little more comprehensible and understandable from the point of view of banking supervision. In the context of the 1999 audit programme, it has been commissioned from our side by the Austrian National Bank to carry out an on-the-spot review of the Eisenstadt Bank and Hypo-Bank Burgenland AG, as it is specifically called Bank Burgenland. That's on the 2nd. In June 1999, the Commission adopted a proposal for a Council Directive on the ==References== The first meeting of experts took place on October 22, 27, 74, 75 of the Banking Act. In particular, this concerns deficiencies in risk management and overall bank management. Moreover, the need for value adjustment for this financial year 1999 has already been assessed as very significant. It's on the 20th. The Oesterreichische National Bank's audit report was officially received on December 1, 1999. Three problem groups were specifically analysed by the staff of my department. First point: What is the risk of fulfilling the Bank's obligations of Burgenland to its creditors? Second point: How does it look like compliance with the regulations of the Banking Act, in particular with regard to the obligation to take care of the management? And third point: Is a criminal assessment of the findings of the test report also given a criminal responsibility? ==References== The National Bank's audit report was also received in writing in January 2000. ==References== At the same time, Januaryen commissioned the Bank Burgenland to provide the Bank's Supervisory Board with this audit report immediately. ==References== In the light of the National Bank's audit report, Januaryen had a meeting with the Bureau of the Bank Burgenland's Supervisory Board, with the result that for the 18th The decision on the appointment of the Executive Board was to be postponed to March 2000 in January 2000. ==References== In March, at the initiative of our department, there was a discussion with the head of state Stix, in which a representative of Bank Austria, who is also a 40-percent owner, participated in the Supervisory Board of Bank Burgenland. The subject of this discussion was the further procedure for the appointment of the Board of Directors, especially because it was felt that it was intended to order Mr Gassner back to five years. Such extension to five years for Director-General Gassner was clearly revealed in the National Bank's report, due to the failure and shortcomings. ==References== In March 2000, the opinion on the audit report of the Oesterreichische Nationalbank, which was received by the bank auditor, shared individual criticism points of the Oesterreichische Nationalbank, but not the statements on the level of the need for value correction. In other words, in March, the auditors did not share this opinion with the National Bank. ==References== In March, there was another meeting with the head of state Stix, again under the supervision of Bank Austria. The position of the Ministry of Finance was reintroduced there after the Supervisory Board meeting on 21 January. In March, one day before that, an extension of the Board of Directors mandate by Directors Gassner was again discussed by five years, with an agreement in which Gassner should have declared his mandate already after a year, which would not have been required by law. That is to say, this solution has also not appeared acceptable to our place of business. That is why it is at the Supervisory Board meeting on 29 October. In March 2000, the agreement was unanimously extended by Director General Gassner until the next Annual General Meeting, i.e. by about one year, and two other Boards, Oswald Koller and Mag. Stagl, in addition to being appointed to the Board of Directors. I should like to point out to you that banking supervision has no possibility of preventing board orders or of direct withdrawal of a member of the board who has already been appointed. One can only initiate a procedure for the appointment of such managing director if one is convinced that the person concerned is unqualified, so it is not a managing director who is able to exercise this essential function as well. ==References== On the other hand, it is a matter for the Member States of the European Union to take the necessary measures in order to ensure that the Community's financial resources are used to finance the development of the internal market. The order was awarded according to § 70 para. 4 Banking Act to immediately restore the law-compliant state and report on the resolution of these defects quarterly. ==References== In May, as determined by the Financial Procuratorate, there was not yet a sufficiently concretely-substantiated crime that would justify an ad obligation to the Procuratorate under § 84 StPO. ==References== June is then for the first time the advertisement of the bank auditor according to § 63 para. 3 Banking Act states that the economic situation of two main borrowers in the absence of audited annual accounts or due to false confirmation notes in the balance sheets of large creditors is unestimable and will therefore delay the balance sheet creation. ==References====External links== On 2 June, two representations of the case were sent to the Public Prosecutor's Office. ==References== On June 21, the Bank's Supervisory Board commissioned Burgenland to clarify the financial situation until June 21. ==References====External links==* Official website In June 2000, the Commission submitted a proposal to the Council for a Council Directive on the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the approximation of the laws, regulations and administrative provisions of the Member States relating to the laws, regulations ==References== For the first time in June 2000, the Bank, which has agreed to vote on the results of the National Bank's audits, has also been informed that the Hom-Rusch Group needs a supply of 2.35 billion schilling. ==References== In June 2000, the Federal Ministry of Finance announced an abstract guarantee of the state of Burgenland, drawn by the head of state Stix, head of state Jellasitz and Bieler state council, amounting to 2.35 billion schilling. Only this guarantee made it possible for the annual accounts to be drawn up and the auditors to be submitted to the audit. Otherwise, it would have been necessary to raise supervision of the bank. I would like to point out that – and I believe that this is an essential point – of course the Executive Board is responsible for all aspects of the loan in this bank and therefore also the careful credit assessment and assessment of the value of collateral falls within the responsibility of the Executive Board. The task of the Supervisory Board is to monitor the management of the Executive Board, which of course also includes the selection, the appointment of the corresponding Executive Board, but also the appointment of this Executive Board. In addition, the Supervisory Board certainly also has the right, if necessary, but also the duty to control the management by taking into account the Bank's business cases or the arrangement of relevant audits by experts. As far as the Supervisory Board agrees to loans, of course he also has the same duty of care as the Executive Board. The Banking Act imposes special audit and warning obligations on bank auditors. They form an integral part of the system of banking supervision in Austria. I should like to point out that, on the basis of the results of the bank Burgenland's on-the-spot audit commissioned by the bank's supervision, the auditors have only seen a targeted examination of Bank Burgenland's credit performance, and that all the annual accounts of the Bank Burgenland of our knowledge have been fully confirmed by the bank auditors since 1991. In my view, the owner of a bank – and especially in the case of the Bank Burgenland – also has a special responsibility in a particular situation, because the country of Burgenland has the full book-view right to this bank on the basis of the country's guarantee liability. By the end of September 1998 – Mr Westenthaler has already done so – the country of Burgenland has also provided the supervisory commissioner of the country with this bank to safeguard the financial interests of the country, and it has thus also had full access to information about the bank. Also for the period after 1998 – I would like to stress this too – § 4 of the Statute of the Bank states that for the duration of the guarantee of default by the Land of Burgenland, the country has the right to any book and operational examination and to any consultation of the other records and documents required for the exercise of obligations and rights. Thus, the representatives of the country still had the right to full information through the bank. I can complement this responsibility of the owner insofar as the owner, through the Supervisory Board orders and in practice on the Supervisory Board orders, has also, of course, also a decisive responsibility regarding the Board of Directors and Supervisory Board. From our point of view, there is a sustainable failure of the system in the present case, obviously at several levels. It is also clear from the facts that only by the intervention of banking supervision – I mentioned it earlier – has the problem been brought to light throughout its scope. Neither the bank auditors nor the board of directors nor the Supervisory Board, nor even the owners, have recognised these problems, let alone taken preventive measures in advance. I should like to point out that there are, of course, a number of legal issues which still need to be resolved. I can inform you that I have joined a renowned law firm and an internationally active audit firm in order to ensure that, in the case of the Bank Burgenland, all necessary measures are taken and taken by our side. As far as the introduction is concerned. I can now come to the specific questions raised. On question 1 – on the question of the liability of the country of Burgenland –: The liability exists, that has been addressed quite correctly. According to § 4 of the Burgenlanden Landesgesetz Nr. 58/1991, as amended by Law No 58/1991 63/1998, the Land of Burgenland is liable as a decommissioning guarantee within the meaning of § 1356 ABGB in the event of the bank's inability to pay for all the liabilities of the Bank. From today's point of view, therefore, it is difficult to say how much this means in the case of the use of taxpayers, because such use depends not only on the amount of liabilities, but also, of course, on the assets and the value of these assets. They know KPMG's opinion, which the company has examined and found that in an approach to decommissionable realisable assets of around 35.7 billion schilling liabilities of around 39.1 billion schillings. That is to say, in this case, we have – as it were of bankruptcy – a burden on the country from the guarantee of default in the magnitude of 3.4 billion schilling. [[Abg. Ing. Westenthaler: 3.5 billion!]] On question 2: "Is it right that the issue of a guarantee can prevent the use of the liability?" By issuing the guarantee, the Executive Board of the Bank was able to draw up the annual accounts for 1999, which was then tested accordingly by the bank auditors. In addition, the Supervisory Board of the Bank has the annual accounts at its meeting of 5. July 2000 – i.e. yesterday. Despite the guarantee given, however, it must be said that, of course, the failure of the country of Burgenland remains unaffected. On question 3, how to assess the guarantee given by the country of Burgenland: There are several legal audits pending today. We ourselves have commissioned one by the Federal Chancellor's Office and by the above mentioned law firm and audit firm. From today's point of view, the guarantee declaration for the bank's security seems to be a legally acceptable procedure. However, I would like to point out that, with regard to the aid and the aid nature, the final decision is, of course, with the European Commission. Question 4: "Is this declaration of guarantee comparable to that of the Landesbank at the time? If no, where is the difference?" The two facts are not comparable from our point of view. There are also differences in the guarantee. I can explain this as follows: The guarantee of the Landesbank was made on the basis of a federal law. In contrast, the Bank Burgenland has a guarantee with the decision of the state government. The term at the Austrian Landesbank was 25 years, and in the case of the Bank Burgenland it is ten years. The guarantee at the Landesbank was accompanied in a second step by an owner's service, i.e. the bank has actually flown money to the extent of the earnings of the loans in need, which has not been agreed with the Bank Burgenland. The Landesbank-Guarantee has not provided for any interest-rate component for interest-rate losses in the guaranteed debt. The reduction of the guarantee by capital increases, receipts from the claims and operating results of the coming years was not available at the Landesbank in this form. On question 5, with the total amount at the Landesbank the taxpayers were asked to check out: We know that the total cost will be around 6 billion schilling in the case of the Landesbank. On question 6: The guarantee declaration shall be drawn up in such a way that the guarantee can only be used in 10 years. In this respect, the country of Burgenland can only be taxed in ten years from the guarantee declaration. However, I should like to point out the following – what I said earlier –: If there were to be a bankruptcy, this would of course mean the burden on the country by the fail guarantee in the order of 3.4 billion schilling immediately. I should also like to point out that even the Board of Directors of the Bank Burgenland assumes in its 1999 financial year that, according to previous experience, a great deal of conviction will be necessary if customer confidence is to be restored – which will be necessary. It is also stated that the projected target for the 2000 financial year, to achieve a operating result of 310 million schilling, must probably be considered very, very ambitious. On Question 7 – Impact on the Stability Pact –: I can say again: The guarantee has no effect on the general economic deficit today. If it is used, that means from our point of view that the amount due would be fully deficit-making. Question 8: "What duties did the supervisory commissioner of the country have, during what period was the head of state Stix active in this function?" The country's supervisory commissioner was originally appointed at the Landes-Hypothekenbank Burgenland-Holding to safeguard the financial interests of the country. That is to say, he had in his responsibility the safeguarding of the financial interests of the country. The head of the state Stix was historically active in various functions at the former mortgage bank Burgenland. There was a change in 1991 with Landesgesetz: The acquisition of the country's default liability for the new stock company was made by this new law, and it was also determined that the supervisory commissioner created at the Landes-Hypothekenbank Burgenland-Holding has full access to the information at Bank Burgenland. What has now sounded somewhat complicated is based on the fact that by the end of 1998, the head of state Stix had full access to the bank's books. I can point out that, according to § 4 of the Bank's Statute, the country of Burgenland has, for the duration of the guarantee of default and thus also after 1998 and to this day, the right to any audit of the accounts, at any time an audit of the operations and at any time consultation of the company's records and supporting documents, as far as this is necessary to safeguard the country's financial interests. On the question 9: "Does it happen that Landesbührer Stix, as Supervisory Commissioner, stayed away from most of the Supervisory Board meetings?" - Yes, that's true. Head of State Stix is away from most of the meetings. Question 10: "How do you assess the fact that owner representatives, national finance officer and supervisory commissioner Stix, despite ongoing indications of irregularities in the case of HOWE, trusted the Chairman of the Board of Directors Gassner as the sole customer caretaker without delay and called for no appropriate control procedure and corresponding to the § 27 Banking Act?" In our view, answering these questions will certainly be the primary task of the Committee of Inquiry set up by the Burgenlander Landtag. However, I have already explained in reply to the previous question that, within the meaning of § 4 of the Statute, the right to book and audit the country at any time was given. As regards the question 11, whether, as a result of the warnings, the Chief of State Stix asked the Supervisory Board for review and, if so, at what time: Head of State Stix has not asked the bank supervision for revisions. Therefore, this was not the case at any time. On the question 12, whether there are any indications or observations that the appointment of the Chairman of the Board of Directors Stix has been carried out: From our point of view, the head of state Stix certainly carried out the re-order of the Director-General Gassner. The bank supervision of my home has immediately notified the Bank Burgenland's audit by the Austrian National Bank to the Supervisory Board of this Bank. Furthermore, in mid-January 2000, the Supervisory Board of the Supervisory Board called for an urgent solution to the problems raised, one point being the non-reposition of the Executive Board. The Supervisory Board then, as already stated at the outset, for the meeting of 18th General Assembly. In January 2000, this item on the agenda, "Chairman affairs". I have already mentioned the discussions that took place with the head of state Stix in this causa. I should also point out that, in our view, this level of information should have been very clear for the country and for the Supervisory Board that the appointment of the Board of Directors of Gassner for five years could only be counterproductive and should not have taken place, that the order for one year was also an absolute compromise option for banking supervision insofar as we did not have the right to withdraw, but could only have initiated a procedure that would most likely have lasted longer than this compromise variant, with which we could then agree on a year. On the question 13: "Is it true that neither the owner of the state of Burgenland nor Bank Austria as the other major shareholder requested the audit of Bank Burgenland by bank supervision or the OeNB?" That's quite right. Neither the owner of the country nor the bank Austria requested the examination of the banking supervision or the Austrian National Bank. Question 14: "It is true that Bank Austria is one of the main shareholders in the Supervisory Board of Bank Burgenland, among others. by DG Randa was represented? If so, what are the causes of Bank Austria from the warnings or warnings? the OeNB report?" It is true that Bank Austria was represented by Director General Randa on the Supervisory Board of Bank Burgenland. ==References====External links== Dr. Drenni nominated as Supervisory Board for Bank Austria on 29 June 1993. June 1993 to 28. May 1997 Dkfm. ==References== ==References====External links== Peter for Bank Austria in the Supervisory Board of Bank Burgenland. On the question of whether there have been incentives on the part of Bank Austria, I must say: There's nothing known to us. There is a report to a member of the Board of Management of Bank Austria on the basis of a discussion of bank supervision with Bank Austria. In addition, nothing is known to us. Question 15: "The basic requirement for proper and effective risk management is its own risk management unit. How do you assess the fact that there was no own risk management facility in the Bank Burgenland?" The audit report of the Oesterreichische National Bank has produced a very critical result in terms of the establishment and conduct of the Bank Burgenland's credit business. There have been serious deficiencies, in particular in the overall risk management, in the risk classification of loans, in computerised processing and in the proper management of transfers and residues as well as in non-profit assets. The Bank was therefore called upon to do so on a discretionary basis by 31 December at the latest. To address the shortcomings in the organisation and to report quarterly on progress. On the question 16: "How do you judge that, among other things, the owner of the country at the Bank Burgenland is extremely dubious basic debt notes of rd. 900 million Schilling accepted as security?" The issue of the value of the collateral taken by the Bank of Burgenland is still the subject of reviews by experts at home and abroad. The assessment of the collateral is certainly the responsibility of the bank institutions, in particular of the directors. However, the Bank itself already assumes that the value of the collateral will be only a fraction of the initial values. However, the problem – and I would also like to point out – is, in principle, that there has been a completely inadequate organisation of the bank's credit assignment. In addition, according to bank operations, loans are normally granted only in exceptional cases in relation to collateral. A credit grant should normally also have been subject to an in-depth credit assessment by the borrower. However, these tests did not take place either on the management side or on the owner's side or only did not take place sufficiently. Even the often quoted "False Testat" should not change this responsibility, because tested balance sheets have only been required at a time when the large credit commitments at risk of the bank have been granted for years. On the question 17, the last question: "What consequences must be drawn from the experience gained by the majority of the bank Burgenland, which is owned by the public authorities, for other banks or banks that are mostly in public hands. Enterprises to be drawn?" In my opinion, the example of the Bank Burgenland shows very clearly that the exercise of the obligations of an owner has not been carried out by the public authorities and the role of ownership has therefore not been sufficiently observed. As a result, it is also very clear that it may not be the task of the public – this is evident from this example, but also from other examples in the past – to act as the owners of credit institutions. In this conviction, the Federal Government and the National Council have also given a privatisation mandate, which includes the full privatisation of the PSC. From our point of view, this is indicative of the future of banking institutions: From my point of view, you should be in private hands. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e20533	Mr President. Mr. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! Through the new EIA law, we achieve essentially three objectives and not, as my predecessors claim, a procedural flood. First of all, there is an extended scope, and that must be so in the sense of the environment. Secondly, it will improve efficiency, allow for faster handling of procedures, and thirdly, we will implement an EU directive that we are already in arrears. In practice, the EIA test shows that there have so far been weaknesses. There has been a lack of acceptance regarding the duration of the proceedings, and too many experts and opinions have been needed. It is said occasionally that this was a so-called directive on expert employment. Of course, there has also been a lot of dissatisfaction, whether there are authorities or companies. The actual evidence that this EIA law is being reformed is that there have been very few procedures and only very few of these procedures have been implemented legally until the end. With the new EIA law, we get flexible transitional arrangements. It is possible to implement the current procedures under the old EIA law or to transfer them to the new EIA law. The standards are maintained and unified. However, there is also help for local decisions. In this context, I would just like to point out that today, if this EIA law concerns a mayor in a municipality, it needs a flexible procedure. But he also has to be able to work with it. It is reasonable for the authorities to deal with this procedure in nine months and in the simplified procedure in six months with the standardised EIA. I do not doubt the objectivity of the authorities as some of the previous speakers do, but I trust in the objectivity of the authorities. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] What surprises me is that it is always claimed that there are cuts being made here. On the contrary! The party position will be extended, whether it be for the localities, the neighbouring communities, the environmental attorneys or the citizens' initiatives. We need less advice. We can come to a proper procedure with final evaluations. It is clear that the EIA is for decision-making. Decisions are always made according to the Matters Act; no law, whether federal or state law, is repealed. Finally, I would like to say: There's nothing going on here. It was all well and well thought out. It's not been over, there's no disassembly, either, but with the tactics that operate the opposition, people, I think, are insecured. Panic is being panicked, a prevention tool is being sought. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] I've heard from your side today: [[towards SPÖ]] Power replaces mind. – To this end, I would like to say the following: You didn't handle the loss of power. We have created and will implement a new EIA law with spirit. Thank you.

Very often suggestions, even improvements, can be taken into consideration in advance through the intensive discussion with those concerned. My experience – reporting from the congregation from which I come – often fails to address these problems in good time. If a preliminary procedure is no longer required at all, then this timeliness is certainly no longer given. From time to time, I have the feeling that companies are working on their investment plans and I believe that if this is done, it will be done without even somehow calculating the administration's running in time. Most of the time, with the application already the permit should be available, then it becomes hectic, then there is pressure on the communities, pressure on the neighbors – and thus the conflict is perfect. In my opinion, this law promotes this situation. Another point would be the division into detailed and principle procedures that a project promoter may require. Once the principle procedure is positive – without any information – it will be difficult to change something in the detailed procedure, or the subsequent detailed discussion will not even have to take place orally. This is a mystery again, because all those who have a party position must then take care of the whole thing themselves. You no longer have the opportunity to inform yourself in an oral proceedings on the spot. That's exactly what I mean, vocations. I believe that this should be discussed again in a large AWG-novelling process. We will be happy to participate in this great novel, AWG 2000. However, I would ask that, as is currently the case in all other legal fields, it should not be announced directly from the Government Bank that it is being negotiated with the SPÖ, but not with those who have a competence in this area and are committed exclusively to the interests of the environment, nature and citizens. - Thank you. ParlaMint-AT_2000-07-06-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00033_d7e23618	Mr President. Mr State Secretary! High house! ==References====External links== In January 1999, we took an essential step towards securing our future. The common European currency, which has reached worldwide acceptance, is based on the same stability principles as the Schilling, but the economic power of the entire euro area is added. The introduction of the euro is not a currency reform, it is a currency changeover. Most older people have already experienced a currency reform and have lost savings. Many are now concerned that the euro also entails money depreciation and loss of assets. It's not like that. All Schilling amounts are converted into euro with an irrevocably fixed exchange rate. This applies both to balances and debts. The present draft law now serves to prepare for the issue of this euro cash and should also have the purpose of adapting the national monetary rules. These are: Reduction of the period of double cash circulation to two months, arrangement of the conversion of the government budget to euro, amendment of the provisions of EU law which are contrary to and repeal of the obsolete monetary law, creation of the legal basis for the issue of euro and cent coins and, ultimately, the inclusion of a further banknote protection provision in the National Bank Act. At the end of the three-year transitional period, the period will now be 1st. Banknotes denominated in euro in January 2002 and coins denominated in euro or in cents. Large amounts of cash must be made available to meet the most modern security standards. In Austria, 470 million euro banknotes and 1.5 billion euro and cent coins are produced with a total weight of 7 500 tonnes. This coin corresponds to the weight of the steel structure of the Eiffel Tower. The two-month period now envisaged is a compromise between the cost of the economy, in particular trade, caused by the double cash circulation and the interest of consumers who are expected to have sufficient time to get used to or to exchange cash. The existing protection provision for euro and cent coins will be reinforced by this current amendment in such a way that, on medals, the designation Euro or Cent is prohibited on its own and not in conjunction with a number or value. As regards the costs of this currency changeover, they shall bear the National Bank, the Austrian Mint, as well as the credit economy and trade. The costs of the Federal Government and the other local authorities are mainly incurred in the areas of computerization and accounting. Finally, ladies and gentlemen, I hope that Mr. and Mrs. Österreicher will end with 1. January 2002 will use the checkcard and the associated Quick function even more. Due to the fact that the smallest light of the "5 Euro" will be in the future, euro and cent coins will increasingly be found in the pockets of the Austrians. Maybe the famous wallet will come back in fashion. - Thank you many times.
